DATE/POOL: 2009-01-06|pool60

We are given a rebalance date of 2009-01-06 with market regime "normal". We need to score each ticker in the long pool and short pool from 0 to 100 based on the strength of mean-reversion setup.

For the long pool (biggest 5-day losers), we want a snap-back setup. Higher score for more oversold, more exhausted seller, more primed for upward reversion. Key indicators: MRL (higher means bigger loser), MRSig (negative of Trail5, so positive for losers), RSI5 (low means oversold), Rng55 (low means near 55-day low, could be support? But for losers, if close to low, might be oversold but also trend is down; need caution), Vel/V (negative means momentum down, but extreme negative might indicate exhaustion? Actually Vel/V is recent return divided by vol, so very negative means strong downward momentum; but for mean reversion, we want exhaustion, so maybe less negative or positive? Actually if Vel/V is very negative, that's strong momentum, not exhaustion. Exhaustion might be when the velocity is slowing or turning positive), %MA100 (negative means below MA, oversold), 52WH (negative, further from high), Mom3m (negative if stock down over 3 months, but we are looking at 5-day losers; negative Mom3m indicates longer-term downtrend, which might not be good for snap-back), AutoC (negative suggests reversal? Actually negative autocorrelation at lag 5 means recent returns tend to reverse, which is good for mean reversion), ADX (low trend strength might mean the move is not trending strongly, better for reversal; high ADX might indicate strong trend that could continue), VolPctl (high vol might mean more extreme moves, but also more risk).

For the short pool (biggest 5-day winners), we want fade setup. Higher score for more overbought, more exhausted buyer, primed for downward reversal. Key indicators: MRS (higher means bigger winner), RSI5 (high means overbought, ideally above 70-80), Rng55 (high means near 55-day high, might be resistance or overextended), Vel/V (positive means strong upward momentum; but we want exhaustion, so maybe not extremely high? Actually if Vel/V is very high, that's strong momentum, not reversal. But we want signs of exhaustion, so maybe Vel/V is positive but not extreme, or maybe it's slowing), %MA100 (positive means above MA, overbought), 52WH (negative? Actually 52WH is % from 52-week high, negative means below high; for winners, they might be still below high, so negative is okay but not overbought relative to high; if 52WH is close to 0, that's near high, more overbought), Mom3m (positive means strong 3-month uptrend, which could support continuation or exhaustion? Mixed), AutoC (negative means reversal tendency), ADX (low trend strength better for reversal), VolPctl (high vol may indicate climactic move).

We need to assign scores from 0 to 100, using the full range. Since we have many indicators, we can combine them into a composite score. Approach: For each pool, we can standardize or rank the indicators and then average with appropriate signs. Since we have cross-sectional percentiles (MRL, MRS) already, we can use them as baselines. But MRL for long pool is high (near 100) for all, since these are the biggest losers. So we need to differentiate within the pool.

We can create a scoring function that penalizes continuation signals and rewards reversal signals.

For long pool:
- MRSig (the magnitude of the drop): larger drop might mean more oversold, but also could be a fundamental event. We'll use it as a factor, but not too heavily since all have large drops. However, within the pool, larger drop might have more bounce potential? Actually from literature, larger moves tend to revert more. So we can give higher score to larger MRSig (Trail5 more negative).
- RSI5: low RSI (<30) is oversold. The lower, the better for bounce. We'll use 100 - RSI5 (since RSI5 is 0-100, lower RSI means higher oversold score). But note some RSI5 are very low like 3.145, 0.0. So we can rank.
- Rng55: how close to 55-day low? Low values mean near low, which might be a support level, but also could be a downtrend. For mean reversion, being at the bottom of the range might be a catalyst for bounce. So lower Rng55 is better? Actually if close to low, it's oversold, but also could break. We'll treat lower as better for long (since it indicates oversold).
- Vel/V: negative means downward velocity. For reversal, we want the velocity to be decelerating or turning positive. Very negative Vel/V might indicate strong downward momentum that could persist. So we want Vel/V to be less negative or positive. Within the long pool, some have positive Vel/V (e.g., SEC_0013 has 0.066). Those might be showing bounce already. So we want Vel/V not too negative. We can rank Vel/V (higher is better for long).
- %MA100: negative and far below MA is oversold, so more negative is better for bounce? But if very far below, it might be in a strong downtrend. Moderately negative might be better. We'll use the absolute value but capped? Actually we want it to be oversold but not extreme. We can use the negative of %MA100 (since %MA100 is negative, negative of that is positive, and larger positive means oversold).
- 52WH: similarly, negative and far from high indicates oversold. So larger negative (more below high) might be better. But again extreme might mean strong downtrend. We'll use -52WH (positive).
- Mom3m: negative means longer-term downtrend. For a snap-back, we want the longer-term trend to be neutral or positive? Actually if stock is in a long-term downtrend, a bounce might be short-lived. So better if Mom3m is less negative or positive. So we want higher Mom3m.
- AutoC: negative autocorrelation at lag 5 means recent returns tend to reverse, so negative is good. We can use -AutoC (positive).
- ADX: low ADX means weak trend, good for reversal. So lower ADX is better. We can use 100 - ADX (normalized, ADX max typically 100 but usually below 60). But we can invert.
- VolPctl: high vol might mean more extreme moves and more likely to revert. But also higher risk. Some studies show that reversals are stronger in high-vol stocks. So higher VolPctl might be good. We'll use VolPctl directly.

We need to combine these into a single score. Since we have many indicators, we can z-score each indicator across the pool, then combine with weights, then rank to 0-100. But we need to ensure the range is used fully. Alternatively, we can rank each indicator from low to high (with appropriate direction) and average the percentiles.

Simpler: For each indicator, assign a percentile rank within the pool (0 to 100) with the direction that favors reversal. Then average those percentiles to get a composite score. This automatically scales 0-100.

Let's define for long pool (60 names):
- MRSig: higher is better (bigger drop → more reversion potential). So rank MRSig high to low, assign percentile (0 to 100) where highest MRSig gets 100.
- RSI5: lower is better (oversold). So rank RSI5 low to high, assign percentile where lowest RSI5 gets 100. But since RSI5 is between 0 and 100, we could also use 100-RSI5. But ranking directly works.
- Rng55: lower is better (near 55-day low). So rank low to high, lowest gets 100.
- Vel/V: higher is better (less negative or positive velocity indicates deceleration). So rank high to low, highest gets 100.
- %MA100: more negative is better (oversold). But note some are positive? Actually all %MA100 in long pool are negative except a few? Check: SEC_0008 has %MA100 14.032 (positive). That means above MA. But it's a loser? Actually its Trail5 is -4.934, so it lost but is above MA. For oversold, being above MA is not oversold. So we want negative and large magnitude. So we can take -%MA100 (positive). Then rank from high to low, higher gets 100.
- 52WH: negative and large magnitude means far from 52-week high. So take -52WH (positive). Rank high to low.
- Mom3m: higher is better (less negative or positive trend). Rank high to low.
- AutoC: negative is better. So take -AutoC (positive). Rank high to low.
- ADX: lower is better (weak trend). So rank low to high, lowest gets 100.
- VolPctl: higher is better (more volatility). Rank high to low.

We can average these 10 percentile ranks to get a composite. Then that composite will be between 0 and 100. However, some indicators might be redundant (e.g., MRSig and Trail5 are same). But we included MRSig. Trail5 is not separate.

We also have MRL and MRS, but they are just percentiles based on MRSig already. So we don't need to include them separately.

For short pool (60 names):
We want fade setup. So:
- MRSig (negative of Trail5, so negative for winners): larger negative (more positive Trail5) means bigger winner. So we want larger Trail5 (positive) for stronger fade. But since MRSig is negative, we can rank Trail5 (positive) high to low, so highest Trail5 gets 100.
- RSI5: high is overbought. So rank RSI5 high to low, highest gets 100. But note many have RSI5=100, so they tie. We need to differentiate.
- Rng55: high means near 55-day high, which is overbought. So rank high to low.
- Vel/V: positive and high means strong upward momentum. But we want exhaustion? Actually very high Vel/V might mean momentum is still strong, not exhausted. For fade, we want the momentum to be decelerating. So we might want Vel/V not extremely high, or maybe we look at other signs. But we can still rank high Vel/V as more overbought? But if it's extremely high, it might be a strong trend that continues. Mean reversion literature suggests that large moves with high velocity are less likely to reverse quickly. So we might want Vel/V to be moderate. However, we have other indicators like AutoC and ADX to capture exhaustion. So I think we still include Vel/V but treat it as: higher Vel/V means stronger momentum, which might be less ideal for reversal. So we want lower Vel/V for fade? Actually if a stock has a huge move but with very high velocity (i.e., it skyrocketed with huge momentum), that might be a news event and could continue. So for fade, we want the move to be sharp but showing signs of exhaustion. So we might want Vel/V to be positive but not extreme. But we don't have a clear direction. Alternatively, we can use the same logic as for long: we want the velocity to be decelerating. For winners, deceleration would mean Vel/V is positive but low or negative? But in the short pool, all Vel/V are positive (since winners). We can look at the sign of Vel/V relative to the overall move? I think we should use the raw Vel/V and interpret high values as strong momentum that might persist, so we want lower Vel/V for fade. That is opposite of long pool. But careful: In long pool, we wanted less negative Vel/V (higher) because we wanted slowing downward momentum. For short pool, we want less positive Vel/V (lower) because we want slowing upward momentum. So for short pool, we rank Vel/V low to high (lowest Vel/V gets 100).
- %MA100: positive and high means above MA, overbought. So rank high to low.
- 52WH: negative means below 52-week high. For fade, being near the 52-week high (i.e., 52WH close to 0) is more overbought. So we want 52WH to be close to 0 (less negative). So we can rank 52WH (which is negative) from high to low (less negative gets 100).
- Mom3m: positive means strong uptrend, could indicate overbought. So rank high to low.
- AutoC: negative is good for reversal (returns tend to reverse). So we want negative AutoC. So rank -AutoC high to low (i.e., more negative AutoC gets higher percentile? Actually we want negative, so more negative is better. So we can compute -AutoC and rank high to low).
- ADX: lower is better (weak trend). So rank low to high.
- VolPctl: higher is better (more volatile, more likely to revert? Or might be climactic). We'll keep as higher is better for fade, so rank high to low.

Also we have MRS which is the percentile of MRSig (negative Trail5). But we can ignore.

We'll average the percentile ranks for short pool similarly.

We need to handle missing values (nan). For SEC_0071, VolPctl is nan. For SEC_0086, VolPctl is nan. For SEC_0089, 52WH is nan and VolPctl is nan. We need to average over available indicators. So for those, we drop the missing indicator from the average.

Given the requirement to output JSON, I need exact scores. I'll do a quick calculation using a weighted sum of percentile ranks. I'll compute an approximate percentile for each indicator and average.

I'll focus on 6 indicators: RSI5_inv (100-RSI5), Rng55_inv (1-Rng55), Vel/V (shifted to positive), AutoC_inv (-AutoC), ADX_inv (100-ADX), VolPctl.

I'll scale each to 0-100 based on min-max.

First, find min and max for each across long pool:

RSI5: min=0, max=51.111 (approx). So RSI5_inv = 100 - RSI5, min=48.889, max=100.

Rng55: min=0.007, max=0.911. Rng55_inv = 1 - Rng55, min=0.089, max=0.993.

Vel/V: min=-0.248, max=0.183. Shift: Vel/V_shifted = Vel/V - min? Actually we want higher better, so (Vel/V - min)/(max-min)*100.

AutoC: min=-0.584, max=0.211. AutoC_inv = -AutoC, min=-0.211, max=0.584. Actually since we want more negative, we can take -AutoC, then min is for max AutoC? Let's compute: AutoC values: min=-0.584, max=0.211. So -AutoC: min=-0.211, max=0.584. Then we want high -AutoC.

ADX: min=10.86, max=53.495. ADX_inv = 100 - ADX, but ADX can go up to 100 but here max 53.5, so ADX_inv min=46.505, max=89.14.

VolPctl: min=31.746, max=96.23.

Now for each ticker, compute average of these scaled. That will give a composite score.

I'll do for a few manually to get sense.

For SEC_0005:
RSI5_inv = 100-3.145=96.855. Scaled: (96.855-48.889)/(100-48.889)*100 = (47.966/51.111)*100 = 93.85
Rng55_inv = 1-0.059=0.941. Scaled: (0.941-0.089)/(0.993-0.089)*100 = (0.852/0.904)*100 = 94.25
Vel/V_shifted = (-0.232 - (-0.248))/(0.183-(-0.248))*100 = (0.016/0.431)*100 = 3.71
AutoC_inv = -0.057? Actually AutoC=0.057, so -AutoC=-0.057. Scaled: (-0.057 - (-0.211))/(0.584-(-0.211))*100 = (0.154/0.795)*100 = 19.37
ADX_inv = 100-15.067=84.933. Scaled: (84.933-46.505)/(89.14-46.505)*100 = (38.428/42.635)*100 = 90.14
VolPctl scaled: (86.111-31.746)/(96.23-31.746)*100 = (54.365/64.484)*100 = 84.32
Average = (93.85+94.25+3.71+19.37+90.14+84.32)/6 = (385.64)/6 = 64.27

So around 64.

For SEC_0024:
RSI5_inv=100-0=100, scaled=100

Vel/V_shifted=(-0.081+0.248)/0.431=0.167/0.431*100=38.75
AutoC_inv=-(-0.377)=0.377, scaled=(0.377+0.211)/0.795=0.588/0.795*100=73.96

Average=(100+75.77+38.75+73.96+92.32+71.07)/6=451.87/6=75.31

So SEC_0024 gets ~75.

Now for short pool, I'll apply similar logic but with opposite directions.

For short pool (winners), we want fade setup. Indicators:
- Trail5 (positive) higher is larger move, better for fade. So rank high.
- RSI5: high is overbought, but many are 100. Need to differentiate by second-level.
- Rng55: high means near 55-day high, overbought. So rank high.
- Vel/V: we want deceleration, so lower Vel/V (less positive) is better for fade. So rank low.
- %MA100: high positive means above MA, overbought. So rank high.
- 52WH: less negative (closer to 0) means near 52-week high, overbought. So rank high (i.e., less negative).
- Mom3m: high positive means strong uptrend, might be overbought. So rank high.
- AutoC: negative is good for reversal. So rank high for more negative (i.e., -AutoC).
- ADX: low is better (weak trend). So rank low.
- VolPctl: high is better (climactic). So rank high.

I'll compute a composite for short pool similarly.

I'll scale each indicator to 0-100 within the short pool.

%MA100: min=-60.604 (SEC_0086), max=26.805 (SEC_0067)

Mom3m: min=-69.62 (SEC_0062), max=30.193 (SEC_0108)

ADX: min=11.863 (SEC_0099), max=71.037 (SEC_0095)
VolPctl: min=76.587 (SEC_0113), max=97.817 (SEC_0084) if we exclude nan. So min=76.587, max=97.817.

I'll use these for scaling.

For short pool, the composite score will be average of scaled indicators:
1. Trail5 scaled: (Trail5 - min)/(max-min)*100
2. RSI5 scaled: (RSI5 - min)/(max-min)*100 (but note higher is better, so standard)
3. Rng55 scaled: (Rng55 - min)/(max-min)*100
4. Vel/V scaled: we want lower better, so (max - Vel/V)/(max-min)*100
5. %MA100 scaled: (max - %MA100? Actually higher %MA100 is better (more positive), so we want higher %MA100. But note %MA100 can be negative. We'll use (max - min) but careful: we want higher %MA100. So ( %MA100 - min )/(max-min)*100.
6. 52WH: we want less negative, i.e., higher value (closer to 0). So (52WH - min)/(max-min)*100 (since all negative, min is most negative, max is least negative).
7. Mom3m: higher is better. So (Mom3m - min)/(max-min)*100.
8. AutoC: we want more negative, so using -AutoC: ( (-AutoC) - min_neg )/(max_neg - min_neg)*100, where min_neg = min of -AutoC = -0.381, max_neg = 0.633. So ( -AutoC - (-0.381) )/(0.633 - (-0.381))*100 = ( -AutoC + 0.381 )/1.014 *100.
9. ADX: lower is better, so (max - ADX)/(max-min)*100.
10. VolPctl: higher is better, so (VolPctl - min)/(max-min)*100.

We'll average these 10 (excluding nan where needed).

I'll compute for a few short tickers to get range.

DATE/POOL: 2009-01-06|pool60

We need to return context_adjustments for each ticker in both pools. The context_adjustment is -3 to +3 based on secondary context (non-Trail5/MRSig/MRL/MRS indicators). We must not use Trail5, MRSig, MRL, MRS as reasons for adjustment. We only use the other columns: RSI5, Rng55, Vel/V, %MA100, 52WH, Mom3m, AutoC, ADX, VolPctl, Market Regime (here "normal"). Also note the BaseScore is given but we are told it's just for reference; we should not use it to adjust. However, the context adjustment is about whether the secondary context strengthens or weakens the setup.

We need to assess for each ticker whether the secondary context supports a mean reversion (improves setup) or not (weakens). For long pool: we want oversold bounce. RSI5 very low is good (exhaustion). Rng55 near 0 might indicate near low, but if too low might be downtrend. Vel/V negative is fine, but if very negative might indicate strong momentum against reversal. %MA100 negative but not too extreme? 52WH negative, but near 52-week low might be value trap. Mom3m negative but not too extreme. AutoC: negative autocorrelation at lag 5 might indicate reversal pattern. ADX high suggests trend strength which might reduce reversal probability. VolPctl high might mean volatile, could snap back but also risky.

For short pool: we want fade. RSI5 high (near 100) is good for overbought. Rng55 near 1 might be near high, but could be resistance. Vel/V positive but not too extreme. %MA100 negative? Actually many stocks are below MA100 despite recent rally, so they are still below MA, which might not be overbought relative to long-term. 52WH negative, so still near 52-week low, which might mean they are still cheap despite rally, could continue. Mom3m negative, so recent rally is within a downtrend, which might be a dead cat bounce. AutoC positive might indicate momentum continuation. ADX high suggests trend, might not reverse. VolPctl high again volatile.

We need to assign -3 to +3 for each, using judgment.

Let's go through each ticker. I'll make a systematic assessment.

First, define rules for long pool:
- RSI5: lower is better for oversold (exhaustion). But note that RSI5 is already low for many. A very low RSI5 (e.g., <20) strengthens. Higher RSI5 (>40) weakens.
- Rng55: if near 0 (close to 55-day low), suggests further downside potential? Actually for long, being near the low might mean it's at support, but if it's a downtrend, could break. Better if it's in the middle or slightly above low? Usually for mean reversion, if close has already bounced from low, that's good. But here Rng55 is close to 0 for some (e.g., RAI 0.059), which might indicate they are at the low of the range, could be deep in downtrend. Perhaps moderate Rng55 (0.3-0.7) is better. Very low might be too weak.
- Vel/V: negative is expected. More negative means stronger recent move relative to vol, could be more oversold, but could also be extreme. Very negative (e.g., -0.2 or less) might be good. Positive Vel/V would be bad because it indicates recent bounce already.
- %MA100: negative means below MA. More negative means deeper below, could be more oversold, but also could be fundamental weakness. Moderate negative (-10 to -20) might be good. Very negative (e.g., -30) might be too deep. However, for mean reversion, being far below MA could snap back. So I'd say more negative is better, but not extremely so.
- 52WH: similar, more negative means near 52-week low, which could be value or distressed. For mean reversion, being near low might increase chance of bounce, but also risk of further decline. I'd say moderate negative is good.
- Mom3m: negative is expected. More negative means strong downtrend, which might not reverse easily. Moderate negative might be better.
- AutoC: negative autocorrelation at lag 5 suggests reversal pattern (recent returns opposite of 5 days ago). Strong negative (e.g., -0.4 or less) is good. Positive autocorrelation suggests momentum.
- ADX: high ADX (>25) suggests strong trend, which might persist, reducing reversal chance. Low ADX (<20) suggests weak trend, more likely to reverse.
- VolPctl: high volatility might increase chances of snap-back but also risk. Moderate might be okay.

For short pool:
- RSI5: high is good (overbought). Near 100 is ideal.
- Rng55: near 1 (close to 55-day high) might be overbought, good for fade. But if already at high, might break out. Moderate might be okay.
- Vel/V: positive is expected. More positive means stronger recent move, could be more overbought, but also extreme. Very positive (e.g., >0.3) is good.
- %MA100: negative means still below MA despite rally. Could mean the rally is within downtrend, might be a dead cat. Positive above MA would be more overbought. So for short, being above MA (positive %MA100) strengthens fade setup? Actually if above MA, it's extended. But many short pool stocks are still far below MA (e.g., ACAS -43.7% below MA). That means they are still in deep downtrend, and the recent rally might be a bounce that could continue. So being negative and far below MA might weaken the fade case because the stock is still cheap in long-term context. Positive or near zero %MA100 would be better for fade.
- 52WH: similarly, still far from 52-week high (negative large) weakens fade, because room to run. Being near 52-week high would strengthen.
- Mom3m: negative means overall downtrend, recent rally might be bounce. More negative might weaken fade. Positive Mom3m would indicate uptrend, strengthening fade.
- AutoC: negative autocorrelation suggests reversal, which could mean the rally might reverse. Positive autocorrelation suggests momentum continuation, weakening fade.
- ADX: high ADX suggests trend, could mean the current move is part of a trend, might continue, weakening fade. Low ADX suggests trendless, more likely to reverse.
- VolPctl: high vol means bigger swings, could snap back.

Now, we need to assign -3 to +3 for each ticker. We should be consistent but not overly systematic. Since there are 120 tickers, we need to do this efficiently. I'll write a Python script in mind, but here I'll manually reason.

====================================================================================================
How To Read The Experiments

Experiment Purpose Guide

Each experiment is useful even when it does not win. This table says what question the test is designed to answer and what kind of insight to extract from it.

ExperimentWhat It TestsUseful Read Even If It FailsCurrent Takeaway
V2 pure LLM vs Trail5Whether the unchanged V2 score prompt adds stock-selection value beyond the deterministic Trail5 baseline under the exact same pool, book, anchor, and accounting.If LLM loses, the prompt is not adding incremental ranking quality; if it wins, the LLM is improving selection inside the reversal pool.V2 LLM beats Trail5 on the key offset 3 lab bench, especially in the ticker-visible historical run and still in the anonymized control.
Ticker-visible vs anonymized V2Whether showing real tickers gives the model helpful or dangerous identity priors versus forcing it to use only the numeric setup features.A performance drop after masking means ticker identity was contributing something; that could be leakage-like prior knowledge or legitimate sector/company context not otherwise provided.Masked V2 still works, but ticker-visible V2 was stronger, so ticker identity likely mattered.
Structured labels / feature attributionWhether forcing the model to name setup labels and primary features improves selection and reveals which inputs it claims to rely on.Even if performance drops, the labels help debug prompt behavior, feature emphasis, and which cited features are associated with good or bad outcomes.Useful diagnostic, weaker as a production prompt; labels are for insight, not proof of internal reasoning.
V2.1 discriminative promptWhether asking for incremental reversal quality beyond Trail5/RSI5 makes the model find subtler setup quality instead of just re-ranking obvious reversal extremes.A failure tells us the prompt over-penalized the core reversal signal or made the model too conservative.It failed; V2.1 moved away from the strongest reversal exposure.
V2.3 rank-only orderingWhether the LLM is better at relative ordering than producing calibrated 0-100 scores.If rank-only loses to score-only, the numeric V2 scoring contract is not just noise; its score scale or calibration still contains useful selection information.It slightly beat Trail5, but did not beat anonymized V2 score-only on CAGR or total return.
V2.4 raw SPY/VIX contextWhether replacing the coarse semantic regime label with raw SPY 200-day-MA and VIX measurements improves the exact frozen no-glossary prompt.A failure would show that raw market context alone does not recover the glossary prompt's primary equal-weight advantage.Completed on 2009-2014: 13.0% CAGR versus 13.2% for Trail5, essentially equal Sharpe, better drawdown, and higher IC. Raw context improved ranking diagnostics but did not add CAGR.
V2.5 balanced outcome memoryWhether past-only outcomes from similar same-side candidates improve V2 scoring beyond a fresh same-run V2 control.The HistRet-only diagnostic separates retrieval quality from the LLM's ability to use the retrieved evidence.The paid paired test is complete. On memory-ready dates V2.5 returned 10.5% CAGR versus 12.2% for fresh V2 and 13.0% for Trail5; drawdown improved to -7.4%.
LLM-long / Trail5-shortWhether the LLM edge is mainly on selecting rebound longs while keeping deterministic shorts fixed.It isolates side contribution; if this works but the reverse does not, future prompt work should focus on the long side.Close to pure V2 but did not beat it; supports that long-side LLM selection carries useful information.
Trail5-long / LLM-shortWhether the LLM edge is mainly on the short book, especially avoiding squeeze/continuation names.A weak result says short-side LLM changes are not enough by themselves or may be less important than long selection.Weaker than pure V2; short-side changes alone were not the main answer.
Top-k LLM swapsWhether small surgical LLM substitutions into a Trail5 book add value without letting the LLM fully control the portfolio.It tells whether LLM alpha is concentrated in a few high-conviction disagreements or requires full-book ranking authority.Helpful diagnostic, but none beat pure V2; LLM seems better as full selector than a tiny patch on Trail5.
Residual LLM scoreWhether the part of the LLM ranking that differs from Trail5 contains independent alpha after removing the obvious Trail5 component.It separates 'LLM is just rediscovering Trail5' from 'LLM has useful disagreement.' Lambda behavior shows how much disagreement the book can tolerate.Some signal exists, but pure V2 still held up better than residual wrappers.
Concentration / book-size curveHow many LLM-ranked names should actually be trusted: 5/5, 10/10, 20/20, 30/30, 40/40, or 60/60.It shows whether the signal is strongest at the very top, needs diversification, or decays slowly through the pool.30/30 remains the best core V2 book; too concentrated gets noisy, too broad dilutes edge.
Long rank curveWhether V2's long ranking actually separates winners from losers across ranks 1-10, 11-20, 21-30, and rejected 31-60 names.If rejected buckets still work, the 30-name cutoff may be arbitrary or too harsh; if rank order declines smoothly, top-30 selection is justified.In masked V2, top 1-10 is strong, 21-30 is weak, and 41-50 rebounds; the curve is not cleanly monotonic.
Broad long core + LLM tiltWhether spreading some long capital across rejected longs improves the portfolio while keeping the V2 top-30 shorts unchanged.It distinguishes alpha from risk smoothing: reserve sleeves may reduce drawdown even if they do not improve CAGR.On masked V2, linear rank decay beats current top-30 on CAGR and Sharpe, but bootstrap evidence is positive rather than decisive.
Side decay / short rank curveWhether the hard short cutoff is justified, and whether linear decay should be applied to longs only, shorts only, or both sides.If short decay lowers CAGR but improves drawdown, the short ranking is doing useful cutoff work while broader shorts mainly diversify risk.On masked V2, long decay wins; short decay reduces CAGR but improves drawdown, and lower short ranks have weaker short P&L.
Anchor/offset robustnessWhether the result depends on one lucky rebalance schedule or survives across all five fixed 5-trading-day offsets.Large offset dispersion warns that timing/seasonality matters and that a single-schedule result should not be overread.Offset 3 is strongest and legacy-aligned; anchor average is more conservative.
Zero-Cost Replay

Broad Long Core + LLM Tilt

This tests whether ticker-masked V2 was too aggressive on the long side by keeping offset 3, the same 60/60 pool, same fixed 5-day C2C accounting, and V2 top-30 shorts unchanged. No LLM calls are used.

Read: this section uses the anonymized V2 control cache, not the historical ticker-visible cache. In the masked run, linear rank-decay across all 60 longs has the best 50/50 CAGR and Sharpe, but the date-block bootstrap is positive rather than decisive. Treat it as a promising wrapper to validate, not a settled replacement.

Zero-Cost Replay

Long Decay vs Short Decay

This tests the ticker-masked V2 cache with decay applied to longs only, shorts only, or both. The short rank curve is measured as short P&L, so positive means the short bucket made money.

Read: linear decay helps when applied to masked V2 longs, but short decay reduces CAGR. Short ranks 31-60 generally have worse short P&L than the top-30 short sleeve, so the hard short cutoff looks more justified than the hard long cutoff.

V2 Inputs

What The V2 LLM Is Asked To Judge

Each score is based on the same feature row per candidate plus the market regime label. The model is effectively asked to synthesize these checks into one side-relative 0-100 mean-reversion setup score.

1 How extreme was the 5-day move?
2 Is the stock oversold/overbought?
3 Is it near range extremes?
4 Is the move fast relative to vol?
5 Is it stretched versus MA / 52-week high?
6 Is medium-term momentum helping or hurting?
7 Does the stock tend to reverse or continue?
8 Is trend strength high?
9 Is current volatility unusually high?
10 What market regime are we in?
Iteration Concepts

Prompt Engineering Concept Board

Each concept is framed as a testable prompt or process lever, not a conclusion.

Score semantics

High

Hypothesis: A soft wording change may sharpen the 5-day target without demoting the core reversal signal.

Why it might help: It points the model directly at the realized holding-window outcome.

Risk: V2.1 showed that a hard residual/incremental wording can suppress the actual edge.

Same as V2 baseline Revise after V2.1 failure

Ranking contract

Medium

Hypothesis: Rank-only or rank+confidence may force cleaner relative ordering than independent scores.

Why it might help: The book is built from cross-sectional ordering, so ranking discipline matters.

Risk: Prior rank+confidence underperformed raw confidence in legacy-style rows.

Same to slightly higher completion Tested / deprioritized

Reasoning visibility

Medium

Hypothesis: Capturing concise rationale labels can reveal why scores diverge from Trail5.

Why it might help: Useful for prompt debugging and manager review.

Risk: More tokens and possible rationalization noise; do only on small samples first.

Higher completion cost Proposed diagnostic

Anti-falling-knife rules

High

Hypothesis: A light caution about continuation/falling-knife setups may reduce bad long-side reversal attempts.

Why it might help: Mean reversion loses when the move is repricing, not exhaustion.

Risk: V2.1 overdid this and removed too many extreme names.

Same as V2 baseline Use only as a soft nudge

Side specialization

High

Hypothesis: Longs and shorts may need different scoring criteria.

Why it might help: Recent diagnostics suggested more lift on longs than shorts.

Risk: Separate rules may reduce comparability and add prompt complexity.

Same if one combined prompt; higher if split calls Proposed

Regime awareness

Medium

Hypothesis: The model may need stricter reversal evidence in crisis/squeeze regimes.

Why it might help: Offset dispersion and year-level variation suggest regime dependence.

Risk: May overcondition on noisy regime labels.

Same as V2 baseline Partially used

Feature framing

Medium

Hypothesis: Better feature wording or grouped feature blocks may help the model use indicators more consistently.

Why it might help: The same raw data can be made easier for the model to parse.

Risk: Prompt bloat increases cost and may distract from Trail5 core.

Same to moderate increase Proposed

Balanced outcome memory

High

Hypothesis: Past-only outcomes from technically similar candidates may help the model distinguish exhaustion from continuation.

Why it might help: It adds empirical analog evidence without changing the original V2 score contract.

Risk: Nearest-neighbor summaries can dominate judgment or encode unstable similarity; require a fresh V2 paired control.

Higher input tokens; same output schema V2.5 paid paired A/B complete; memory hurt CAGR

Model / hyperparameters

Medium

Hypothesis: Reasoner vs chat/flash and temperature may change ranking quality and cost.

Why it might help: This is the cleanest cost/performance tradeoff test once the harness is locked.

Risk: Changing model breaks direct comparability with existing DeepSeek runs.

Variable Proposed A/B
Next Tests

Experiment Gameplan

All prompt A/Bs should keep the same locked harness unless the experiment explicitly says otherwise: offset 3, 60/60 -> 30/30, gross fixed 5-day C2C, paired Trail5 baseline.

PriorityExperimentPrompt changeHarnessSuccess read
1V2 unchanged controlNoneoffset 3, 60/60 -> 30/30, gross 5-day C2CAnchor/lab control for all prompt A/Bs
2V2.2 soft multi-feature nudgeKeep V2 wording; add one line: Trail5/RSI5 matter, but other features can confirm or contradict the setupSame as controlKeeps V2 score/Trail5 relationship while improving IC or drawdown
3V2.3 rank-only orderingKeep the V2 setup framing but ask for complete ordered lists instead of 0-100 scoresSame as controlTests whether ordinal judgment beats numeric score calibration
4V2.4 raw market context (complete)Keep the frozen no-glossary prompt exact; replace regime label with raw SPY 200-day-MA and VIX measurementsSame as control; pair with frozen no-glossary scoresMeasured near-flat versus Trail5, with better IC/drawdown but no CAGR lift
5V2.5 balanced outcome memory (complete)Keep current V2 contract; add past-only HistN, HistWin, and HistRet analog summariesSame as control; fresh V2, Trail5, and HistRet-only paired controlsDid not pass: memory-ready CAGR was -1.7pp versus fresh V2 and -2.6pp versus Trail5, despite a smaller drawdown
6Expected 5-day reversal strengthDefine score as setup strength for the next 5 trading days without saying to ignore Trail5/RSI5Same as controlCAGR/Sharpe lift without worse drawdown
7Soft continuation cautionOne caution line about repricing/falling-knife/squeeze risk; no feature-specific hard gatesSame as controlImproves long-side contribution and drawdown without killing Trail5 exposure
8Long/short specialized criteriaUse separate criteria for snap-back longs and fade shorts inside same JSON contractSame as controlSide attribution shows lift on the weaker side without hurting the stronger side
9Ticker identity auditCompare ticker-visible, anonymized, and shuffled ticker-label score stability; optional label audit includes a ticker_identity optionSame as control; preferably score-only first, labels on a small sampleIf shuffled names move scores materially, ticker identity is influencing the model
10Small-sample explanation captureAdd short rationale labels for 20-30 dates onlyDiagnostic sample, not production scoringClear failure taxonomy for next prompt iteration
